<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We classify the prime and radical ideals of the Lubin–Tate deformation ring invariant under an open Morava stabilizer subgroup, at every prime and positive height. This proves Chai's invariant-ideal conjecture in the standard finite-residue-field formulation. It also proves the Hovey–Strickland conjecture: the dualizable $`K(n)`$-local category has exactly $`n+2`$ thick tensor ideals, and its Balmer spectrum is a chain of $`n+1`$ points.

- **claim-invariant-radicals** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-invariant-radicals)): Let $U\subseteq A_n^\times$ be any open subgroup. The proper prime ideals of $E_0$ stabilized by $U$ are precisely $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n.$$ The $U$-invariant radical ideals are precisely $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n,\ E_0.$$ The latter list also gives the radical ideals invariant under the full canonical $G_n$-action.
  - Explanation: For every open subgroup of the Morava stabilizer group, the only invariant primes of the deformation ring are zero and the height ideals. The invariant radical ideals are these together with the unit ideal, and the same list holds for the full extended stabilizer.
- **claim-hovey-strickland** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every prime $p$ and every $n\geq 1$, the thick tensor ideals of $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$ are exactly $$\mathcal D_{(n,p)}=\mathcal D_0 \supsetneq\mathcal D_1\supsetneq\cdots \supsetneq\mathcal D_n\supsetneq\mathcal D_{n+1}=0.$$ In particular, there are $n+2$ such ideals, and each $\mathcal D_k$ is independent of the chosen finite type-$k$ spectrum.
  - Explanation: The dualizable K(n)-local category has a finite strictly decreasing chain of thick tensor ideals, each generated by a finite spectrum of the corresponding type, and there are no others.
- **claim-frobenius-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-formal-separation)): Let $h,r\geq1$, put $q=p^h$, and let $(b_\ell)_{\ell\in\mathbb N^r}$ be an arbitrary family in $C$. Suppose $s\in\mathfrak m_C^r$ satisfies, for every integer $m\geq0$, $$\label{formal:root-bound} \limsup_{N\longrightarrow\infty} \left\|\sum_{\|\ell\|\leq m} b_\ell^{1/q^N}s^\ell\right\| \leq \\|s\\|^{m+1}.$$ Then, for every $k$-linear functional $\lambda:C\to k$, $$\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb N^r}\lambda(b_\ell)s^\ell=0.$$ The series in this conclusion converges, and no continuity assumption on $\lambda$ is required.
  - Explanation: If asymptotic Frobenius-root bounds hold for the truncated sums, then every k-linear functional applied to the coefficients gives a vanishing convergent series, with no continuity needed.
- **claim-reduced-smooth** (theorem, [source](#stmt-formal-reduced-smooth)): Let $\mathcal G$ be a smooth formal group over $k$ of dimension $r$, with coordinate ring $R=k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$. If $\mathcal H\subseteq\mathcal G$ is a reduced closed formal subgroup, then $\mathcal H$ is smooth. More precisely, for $B=R/I$, with augmentation ideal $\mathfrak m_B$, put $e=\dim_k(\mathfrak m_B/\mathfrak m_B^2)$. Then $B\cong k[[X_1,\ldots,X_e]]$ as a complete local $k$-algebra.
  - Explanation: A reduced closed formal subgroup of a smooth formal group over a perfect field is smooth, and its coordinate ring is a power series ring in the embedding dimension.
- **claim-formal-annihilator** (theorem, [source](#stmt-formal-annihilator)): Let $h,r\geq1$. Every proper reduced closed formal subgroup $\mathcal H\subsetneq\Gamma_h^r$ is annihilated by a nonzero homomorphism $\Gamma_h^r\to\Gamma_h$ defined over $k$.
  - Explanation: Each proper reduced closed formal subgroup of a power of a Honda group is killed by a nonzero homomorphism to the Honda group defined over the residue field.
- **claim-formal-closure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-formal-closure)): Let $h,r\geq1$, and let $S$ be a subgroup of $\Gamma_h^r(\mathfrak m_C)$. If a nonzero power series in $k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$ vanishes on $S$, then there are $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in A_h$, not all zero, such that $$a_1(s_1)+_{\Gamma_h}\cdots+_{\Gamma_h}a_r(s_r)=0 \qquad\text{for every }s\in S.$$
  - Explanation: A nonzero formal equation vanishing on a subgroup of Honda-group points yields a nonzero endomorphism relation among the coordinates that holds on the whole subgroup.
- **claim-ff-curve-geometry** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bundles-geometry)): The curve $X_C$ is integral, regular and Noetherian of dimension one, and $H^0(X_C,\mathcal O_{X_C})=\mathbb Q_p$. Degrees of vector bundles are integers, and every nonzero effective divisor has positive degree. For each $s\geq1$ there is a stable bundle $$\mathcal E_s=\mathcal O_{X_C}(1/s)$$ of rank $s$ and degree one.
  - Explanation: The Fargues–Fontaine curve is an integral regular Noetherian curve with constants the p-adic numbers, degrees are integers, and stable bundles of degree one exist in each rank.
- **claim-generic-independence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bundles-independence)): Let $1\leq r<n$, and let $z_1,\ldots,z_r\in H^0(X_C,\mathcal E_n)$ be linearly independent over $\mathbb Q_p$. Their images in the generic fiber of $\mathcal E_n$ are linearly independent over the function field $K_X$ of $X_C$.
  - Explanation: Fewer than n global sections of the degree-one stable bundle of rank n that are independent over the p-adic numbers stay independent over the function field of the curve.
- **claim-section-sheaf-dictionary** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bundles-input)): 1. *For every $s\geq1$, after choosing an identification of a height-$s$ Lubin–Tate special fiber with $\Gamma_s$, there is a natural isomorphism of $\mathbb Q_p$-vector space sheaves $$\mathcal H_s\ \cong\ \mathcal B(\mathcal E_s).$$ In particular, its value at $\operatorname{Spa}(C,\mathcal O_C)$ identifies $\Gamma_s(\mathfrak m_C)$ with $H^0(X_C,\mathcal E_s)$.* 2. *For vector bundles $\mathcal V,\mathcal W$ on $X_C$ with nonnegative Harder–Narasimhan slopes, the natural map $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{X_C}(\mathcal V,\mathcal W) \longrightarrow \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{\operatorname{Sh}_{\mathrm{Ab}}(\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}_C)} \bigl(\mathcal B(\mathcal V),\mathcal B(\mathcal W)\bigr)$$ is an isomorphism. Thus every additive sheaf morphism between these section sheaves is induced by a unique bundle morphism.
  - Explanation: Honda group sheaves are naturally isomorphic to relative section sheaves of the stable bundles, and additive sheaf maps between section sheaves of nonnegative-slope bundles come from unique bundle maps.
- **claim-nondegeneracy** (theorem, [source](#stmt-bundles-nondegeneracy)): Let $0<h<n$ and $1\leq r<n$. Suppose that $z_1,\ldots,z_r\in\mathcal H_n(C)$ are $\mathbb Q_p$-linearly independent and that $$\nu:\mathcal H_n\longrightarrow\mathcal H_h$$ is a nonzero morphism of $\mathbb Q_p$-vector space sheaves on $\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}_C$. Then the subgroup $$S_\nu=\bigl\{\bigl(\nu(dz_1),\ldots,\nu(dz_r)\bigr): d\in A_n\bigr\} \subseteq\Gamma_h^r(\mathfrak m_C)$$ satisfies no nonzero formal power series equation over $k$.
  - Explanation: For independent points and a nonzero sheaf morphism to a lower-height Honda sheaf, the orbit subgroup satisfies no nonzero formal power series equation over the residue field.
- **claim-algebraization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-algebraization)): The finite group schemes $$\mathcal G[p^j] =\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}\bigl(R_0[[T]]/([p^j]_H(T))\bigr),\qquad j\ge1,$$ form an algebraic $p$-divisible group $\mathcal G$ of height $n$ over $R_0$. At the point $x$, its connected formal group is $H_x$ and its étale height is $r=n-h$. All the $C$-valued points of every level have coordinate in $\mathfrak m_C$.
  - Explanation: The finite kernels of the universal p-series form an algebraic p-divisible group of height n over the characteristic-p deformation ring, whose connected part at the chosen point is the evaluated formal group and whose étale part has the complementary height.
- **claim-specialization** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-transport)): For each parameter $y$ in the height-at-least-$h$ polydisc, the formula $$\Theta_y(z)=\lim_{j\to\infty} P_y^{\circ j}\bigl(z^{1/Q^j}\bigr) \label{def:theta-limit}$$ defines a continuous natural homomorphism $$\Theta_y:\mathcal H_n(R)\longrightarrow H_y(R^{\circ\circ}).$$ Here the roots are unique because $R$ is perfect, and $P_y^{\circ j}$ denotes composition. The map $$\Phi_y:z\longmapsto \bigl(\Theta_y(p^{-m}z)\bigr)_{m\ge0} \label{def:cover-identification}$$ is a natural isomorphism $$\mathcal H_n(R)\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \varprojlim_{[p]_{H_y}} H_y(R^{\circ\circ}),$$ and $\Theta_y$ is its zeroth projection. For each fixed $z\in\mathcal H_n(C)$, the function $y\mapsto\Theta_y(z)$ is analytic on the parameter polydisc.
  - Explanation: A limit of iterated p-series on roots defines a continuous natural homomorphism from the perfectoid Honda sheaf to the evaluated formal group, identifies the sheaf with the universal cover, and depends analytically on the parameter.
- **claim-tate-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-tate-kernel)): At the chosen point $x$, the map $\Theta_x$ is surjective on $C$-points, and $$T_x=\ker\bigl(\Theta_x:\mathcal H_n(C)\to H_x(\mathfrak m_C)\bigr) \cong\mathbb Z_p^r.$$ In particular, a $\mathbb Z_p$-basis $z_1,\ldots,z_r$ of $T_x$ is $\mathbb Q_p$-linearly independent in $\mathcal H_n(C)$.
  - Explanation: The specialization map at the chosen point is surjective on points and its kernel is a free module over the p-adic integers whose basis is independent over the p-adic numbers.
- **claim-kodaira-spencer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-ks)): The first-order parameter deformation of $\mathcal G$ induces an isomorphism of free $R_0$-modules $$\mathsf K:R_0^{n-1}\xrightarrow{\ \sim\ } \mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_{R_0}(\omega_{\mathcal G^\vee},\mathop{\mathrm{Lie}}\mathcal G).$$ For every small $C$-valued parameter point $x$, its specialization is the linear isomorphism from parameter directions to marked first-order deformations of $\mathcal G_x$, with the constant deformation as origin.
  - Explanation: First-order parameter variations correspond isomorphically to marked first-order deformations of the algebraic p-divisible group, and this persists at every small point.
- **claim-height-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-height-rigidity)): Let $A$ be a local Artin $C$-algebra with residue field $C$, and let $F/A$ be a marked deformation of a height-$h$ formal group over $C$. If $[p]_F(T)$ factors through $T^{p^h}$ over $A$, then $F$ is the constant deformation, with its marking.
  - Explanation: A marked Artinian deformation of a formal group of fixed height whose p-series still factors through the p^h-th power is trivial.
- **claim-tate-coordinates** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-coordinates)): For a $\mathbb Z_p$-basis $z_1,\ldots,z_r$ of $T_x$, the analytic map $$\theta(y)=\bigl(\Theta_y(z_1),\ldots,\Theta_y(z_r)\bigr) \label{def:theta}$$ has invertible differential at $x$ on the height-at-least-$h$ polydisc.
  - Explanation: The specializations of a Tate basis give analytic coordinates on the height stratum whose differential at the base point is invertible.
- **claim-coefficient-recursion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-recursion)): Let $B$ be a characteristic-$p$ algebra, let $f(Z)=bZ+c_2Z^2+\cdots\in B[[Z]]$, and put $q=p^h$. For $\alpha(T)=\sum_{j\ge1}a_jT^j$, the identity $$\alpha(f(T^q))=\alpha(T)^q$$ holds if and only if, for every $j\ge1$, $$a_j^q-b^ja_j =\sum_{i<j}a_i[Z^j]f(Z)^i. \label{def:coefficient-recursion}$$ If $B=C$, all coefficients of $f$ are integral, and the coefficients $a_j$ satisfy these equations, then all $a_j$ are integral. In particular, after prescribing any finite initial segment satisfying the equations, every recursive extension by roots in $C$ has integral coefficients.
  - Explanation: A series intertwines the p-series with Frobenius powers exactly when its coefficients satisfy an explicit recursion, and integral data force integral coefficients.
- **claim-natural-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-natural-comparison)): Choose a formal group isomorphism $\alpha_x:H_x\xrightarrow{\sim}\Gamma_h$ over $C$. Its forward coefficients are integral, and composition gives a nonzero natural $\mathbb Q_p$-linear morphism of sheaves on perfectoid spaces over $C$, $$\nu_x:\mathcal H_n\longrightarrow\mathcal H_h, \qquad \nu_x(z)=\alpha_x(\Theta_x(z)). \label{def:nu}$$
  - Explanation: Composing specialization with an integral formal isomorphism gives a nonzero natural linear morphism from the height-n Honda sheaf to the height-h Honda sheaf.
- **claim-continuous-action** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-continuous-action)): Put $\mathfrak m_E=(p,u_1,\ldots,u_{n-1})\subset E_0$. For $M\geq1$ and $N\geq M$, every element of $1+p^NA_n$ acts trivially on $E_0/\mathfrak m_E^M$. Consequently the stabilizer action on $E_0$, and on its characteristic-$p$ quotient, is adically continuous.
  - Explanation: Sufficiently deep congruence subgroups act trivially on finite-order quotients of the deformation ring, so the stabilizer action is adically continuous.
- **claim-stabilizer-transport** (theorem, [source](#stmt-def-stabilizer)): For $g\in A_n^\times$ and $z\in\mathcal H_n(C)$ one has $$\Theta_{y_g}(gz)=\iota_g(\Theta_x(z)). \label{def:orbit-transport}$$ Consequently $gT_x=T_{y_g}$.
  - Explanation: Specialization is equivariant for the stabilizer action, so group elements carry the Tate lattice at the base point onto the Tate lattice at the translated point.
- **claim-local-orbit-density** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-density)): Fix $1\leq h<n$ and a $C$-valued point $x$ of the parameter polydisc $I_h=0$ with $u_h(x)\neq0$. For $g\in A_n^\times$, write $y_g$ for its orbit point, with the transport convention of Lemma <a href="#def:stabilizer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:stabilizer">21</a>. Let $G$ be any open subgroup of $A_n^\times$. If an analytic germ $P$ at $x$ on the parameter polydisc defined by $I_h=0$ vanishes at all points of the orbit $\{y_g:g\in G\}$ sufficiently close to $x$, then $P=0$.* *It suffices to assume that, for every $d\in A_n$, $P(y_{1-p^Nd})=0$ for all sufficiently large $N$.
  - Explanation: On each positive-characteristic height stratum, an analytic germ vanishing on an open-subgroup orbit near the base point is identically zero.
- **claim-finite-jets** (theorem, [source](#stmt-orb-finite-jets)): Let $F$ be a complete nonarchimedean valued field, let $r\geq1$, and use the maximum norm on $F^r$. Let $\theta=(\theta_i)_{i=1}^r$ be an analytic germ at $0\in F^r$ with $\theta(0)=0$, and let $a_j$, for $j\geq1$, be scalar analytic germs at $0$. Define formal series $$U_i(X)=\sum_{j\geq1}a_j(X)\theta_i(X)^j \quad\text{in }F[[X_1,\ldots,X_r]],$$ using the Taylor expansions of the germs. Suppose that the linear part $L=dU_0$ is invertible.* *Let $\delta_N\to0$ in $F^r$, and suppose that coefficients $a_{N,j}\in\mathcal O_F$ have been chosen for every $N$ and $j\geq1$ so that, for every $M\geq1$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$a_{N,j}=a_j(\delta_N)\qquad(1\leq j\leq M).$$ This includes the requirement that the finitely many germs in this formula are defined at $\delta_N$. For sufficiently large $N$, put $$U_{N,i}=\sum_{j\geq1}a_{N,j}\theta_i(\delta_N)^j.$$ These numerical series converge. If $P$ is an analytic germ with $P(\delta_N)=0$ eventually, write its unique formal expansion as $$P(X)=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb N^r}B_\ell U(X)^\ell.$$ Then there is a constant $D>0$ such that $\\|\delta_N\\|\leq D\\|U_N\\|$ for all sufficiently large $N$. Moreover, for every $m\geq0$ there is $D_m>0$ such that $$\label{orb:finite-jet-estimate} \left\|\sum_{\|\ell\|\leq m}B_\ell U_N^\ell\right\| \leq D_m\\|U_N\\|^{m+1}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. The threshold and $D_m$ may depend on $m$. No convergence of the full formal series $U$ or $\sum_\ell B_\ell Z^\ell$ is required.
  - Explanation: Matching finitely many coefficient germs along a sequence tending to the base point turns formal expansion identities into quantitative finite-jet estimates, with no convergence of the full formal series needed.
- **claim-auxiliary-curve** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fin-curve)): Let $R$ be a complete Noetherian local domain with residue field $k_n$.* 1. *Suppose $R$ is a local $k_n$-algebra and $0\ne a\in\mathfrak m_R$. For every $c\in\mathfrak m_C\setminus\{0\}$, there is a continuous $k_n$-algebra homomorphism $$R\longrightarrow\mathcal O_C$$ sending $a$ to $c$ and $\mathfrak m_R$ into $\mathfrak m_C$.* 2. *Suppose $R$ is a local $W(k_n)$-algebra and $p\ne0$ in $R$. Fix the usual embedding $W(k_n)\hookrightarrow\mathcal O_{\mathbb C_p}$. There is a continuous $W(k_n)$-algebra homomorphism $$R\longrightarrow\mathcal O_{\mathbb C_p}$$ sending $\mathfrak m_R$ into $\mathfrak m_{\mathbb C_p}$.* *Neither homomorphism is asserted to be injective.
  - Explanation: A complete Noetherian local domain admits continuous homomorphisms to the integers of the relevant complete field, in equal and mixed characteristic, landing in the maximal ideal; injectivity is not claimed.
- **claim-bounded-translation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fin-translation)): Let $L$ be a complete nontrivially valued nonarchimedean field, let $r\ge0$, and let $$\mathscr B_r(L)= \left\{\sum_{I\in\mathbb N^r}a_I T^I: \sup_I\|a_I\|<\infty\right\}.$$ Equip this algebra with the coefficient norm $\\|f\\|=\sup_I\|a_I\|$. For $x\in L^r$ with $\\|x\\|<1$, substitution $T\mapsto x+T$ defines an isometric $L$-algebra automorphism $\tau_x$ of $\mathscr B_r(L)$, with inverse $\tau_{-x}$. In particular, a nonzero bounded-coefficient series has a nonzero analytic germ at every such $x$.
  - Explanation: Translation by a small point is an isometric automorphism of the bounded-coefficient series algebra, so a nonzero bounded series has a nonzero germ at every small point.
- **claim-charzero-orbit-germs** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fin-charzero)): Let $U\subseteq A_n^\times$ be an open subgroup and $x\in\mathfrak X_{\mathbb C_p}(\mathbb C_p)$. If an analytic germ at $x$ vanishes at $g x$ for every $g\in U$ sufficiently close to the identity, that germ is zero.
  - Explanation: In characteristic zero, an analytic germ vanishing along an open-subgroup orbit near the identity is zero, proved through the Gross–Hopkins period map.
- **claim-invariant-primes** (classification, [source](#stmt-fin-primes)): Every proper prime ideal of $E_0$ stable under an open subgroup of $A_n^\times$ is one of $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n.$$
  - Explanation: Each proper prime ideal of the deformation ring stable under an open subgroup is zero or a height ideal.
- **claim-radical-ideals** (classification, [source](#stmt-fin-radicals)): For every open subgroup $U\subseteq A_n^\times$, the $U$-invariant radical ideals of $E_0$ are precisely $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n,\ E_0.$$ The same list gives the radical ideals invariant under the full canonical extended stabilizer group $G_n$.
  - Explanation: The invariant radical ideals are exactly zero, the height ideals and the unit ideal, for every open subgroup and for the extended stabilizer.
- **claim-support-comparison** (theorem, [source](#stmt-fin-support)): For every prime $p$ and height $n\ge1$, the following hold.* 1. *The functor $\mathcal F:\mathcal D\to\mathop{\mathrm{Perf}}(E)$ is exact and symmetric monoidal, where the source uses the $K(n)$-localized product. It detects tensor-nilpotence of morphisms.* 2. *The set $S(X)$ is a closed $G_n$-invariant subset of $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(E_0)$, and $$S(X\otimes Y)=S(X)\cap S(Y).$$ For $X,Y\in\mathcal D$, $$X\in\langle Y\rangle_\otimes \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad S(X)\subseteq S(Y).$$* 3. *For any finite $p$-local spectrum $F(k)$ of type $k$, $0\le k\le n$, $$S\bigl(L_{K(n)}F(k)\bigr)=V(I_k).$$ Also $S(0)=\varnothing$ and $S(L_{K(n)}S)=\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(E_0)$.
  - Explanation: Base change to Morava E-theory is exact, symmetric monoidal and detects tensor nilpotence; the induced support is closed, invariant and multiplicative, characterizes ideal membership, and equals the height locus on finite type generators.
- **claim-balmer-spectrum** (classification, [source](#stmt-fin-balmer)): For every prime $p$ and every $n\geq1$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Spc}}(\mathcal D_{(n,p)}) =\{\mathcal D_1,\ldots,\mathcal D_{n+1}\},\qquad \overline{\{\mathcal D_j\}} =\{\mathcal D_l:j\leq l\leq n+1\}.$$ Thus $\mathcal D_1$ is the generic point and $\mathcal D_{n+1}=0$ is the unique closed point. For $0\leq k\leq n$, $$\operatorname{supp}(L_{K(n)}F(k)) =\{\mathcal D_j:k<j\leq n+1\}.$$
  - Explanation: The Balmer spectrum consists of the proper members of the ideal chain, ordered by inclusion, with the first as generic point and the zero ideal as the closed point, and the supports of finite type generators are given explicitly.

        "anchor": "stmt-bundles-input",
        "exact_quote": "1.  *For every $s\\geq1$, after choosing an identification of a height-$s$ Lubin–Tate special fiber with $\\Gamma_s$, there is a natural isomorphism of $\\mathbb Q_p$-vector space sheaves $$\\mathcal H_s\\ \\cong\\ \\mathcal B(\\mathcal E_s).$$ In particular, its value at $\\operatorname{Spa}(C,\\mathcal O_C)$ identifies $\\Gamma_s(\\mathfrak m_C)$ with $H^0(X_C,\\mathcal E_s)$.*\n\n2.  *For vector bundles $\\mathcal V,\\mathcal W$ on $X_C$ with nonnegative Harder–Narasimhan slopes, the natural map $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{X_C}(\\mathcal V,\\mathcal W)\n      \\longrightarrow\n      \\mathop{\\mathrm{Hom}}_{\\operatorname{Sh}_{\\mathrm{Ab}}(\\mathop{\\mathrm{Perf}}_C)}\n           \\bigl(\\mathcal B(\\mathcal V),\\mathcal B(\\mathcal W)\\bigr)$$ is an isomorphism.         "anchor": "stmt-def-recursion",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be a characteristic-$p$ algebra, let $f(Z)=bZ+c_2Z^2+\\cdots\\in B[[Z]]$, and put $q=p^h$. For $\\alpha(T)=\\sum_{j\\ge1}a_jT^j$, the identity $$\\alpha(f(T^q))=\\alpha(T)^q$$ holds if and only if, for every $j\\ge1$, $$a_j^q-b^ja_j\n       =\\sum_{i<j}a_i[Z^j]f(Z)^i.\n \\label{def:coefficient-recursion}$$ If $B=C$, all coefficients of $f$ are integral, and the coefficients $a_j$ satisfy these equations, then all $a_j$ are integral. In particular, after prescribing any finite initial segment satisfying the equations, every recursive extension by roots in $C$ has integral coefficients.",
        "source_id": "PM-311.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-def-recursion",

      "exact_statement": "Let $B$ be a characteristic-$p$ algebra, let $f(Z)=bZ+c_2Z^2+\\cdots\\in B[[Z]]$, and put $q=p^h$. For $\\alpha(T)=\\sum_{j\\ge1}a_jT^j$, the identity $$\\alpha(f(T^q))=\\alpha(T)^q$$ holds if and only if, for every $j\\ge1$, $$a_j^q-b^ja_j\n       =\\sum_{i<j}a_i[Z^j]f(Z)^i.\n \\label{def:coefficient-recursion}$$ If $B=C$, all coefficients of $f$ are integral, and the coefficients $a_j$ satisfy these equations, then all $a_j$ are integral. In particular, after prescribing any finite initial segment satisfying the equations, every recursive extension by roots in $C$ has integral coefficients.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orb-density",
        "exact_quote": "Fix $1\\leq h<n$ and a $C$-valued point $x$ of the parameter polydisc $I_h=0$ with $u_h(x)\\neq0$. For $g\\in A_n^\\times$, write $y_g$ for its orbit point, with the transport convention of Lemma <a href=\"#def:stabilizer\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"def:stabilizer\">21</a>. Let $G$ be any open subgroup of $A_n^\\times$. If an analytic germ $P$ at $x$ on the parameter polydisc defined by $I_h=0$ vanishes at all points of the orbit $\\{y_g:g\\in G\\}$ sufficiently close to $x$, then $P=0$.*\n\n*It suffices to assume that, for every $d\\in A_n$, $P(y_{1-p^Nd})=0$ for all sufficiently large $N$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-orb-finite-jets",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a complete nonarchimedean valued field, let $r\\geq1$, and use the maximum norm on $F^r$. Let $\\theta=(\\theta_i)_{i=1}^r$ be an analytic germ at $0\\in F^r$ with $\\theta(0)=0$, and let $a_j$, for $j\\geq1$, be scalar analytic germs at $0$. Define formal series $$U_i(X)=\\sum_{j\\geq1}a_j(X)\\theta_i(X)^j\n \\quad\\text{in }F[[X_1,\\ldots,X_r]],$$ using the Taylor expansions of the germs. Suppose that the linear part $L=dU_0$ is invertible.*\n\n\n*Let $\\delta_N\\to0$ in $F^r$, and suppose that coefficients $a_{N,j}\\in\\mathcal O_F$ have been chosen for every $N$ and $j\\geq1$ so that, for every $M\\geq1$, all sufficiently large $N$ satisfy $$a_{N,j}=a_j(\\delta_N)\\qquad(1\\leq j\\leq M).$$ This includes the requirement that the finitely many germs in this formula are defined at $\\delta_N$. For sufficiently large $N$, put $$U_{N,i}=\\sum_{j\\geq1}a_{N,j}\\theta_i(\\delta_N)^j.$$ These numerical series converge. If $P$ is an analytic germ with $P(\\delta_N)=0$ eventually, write its unique formal expansion as $$P(X)=\\sum_{\\ell\\in\\mathbb N^r}B_\\ell U(X)^\\ell.$$ Then there is a constant $D>0$ such that $\\|\\delta_N\\|\\leq D\\|U_N\\|$ for all sufficiently large $N$. Moreover, for every $m\\geq0$ there is $D_m>0$ such that $$\\label{orb:finite-jet-estimate}\n \\left|\\sum_{|\\ell|\\leq m}B_\\ell U_N^\\ell\\right|\n \\leq D_m\\|U_N\\|^{m+1}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. The threshold and $D_m$ may depend on $m$. No convergence of the full formal series $U$ or $\\sum_\\ell B_\\ell Z^\\ell$ is required.",
        "source_id": "PM-311.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

For sufficiently large $N$, put $$U_{N,i}=\\sum_{j\\geq1}a_{N,j}\\theta_i(\\delta_N)^j.$$ These numerical series converge. Moreover, for every $m\\geq0$ there is $D_m>0$ such that $$\\label{orb:finite-jet-estimate}\n \\left|\\sum_{|\\ell|\\leq m}B_\\ell U_N^\\ell\\right|\n \\leq D_m\\|U_N\\|^{m+1}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. The threshold and $D_m$ may depend on $m$. No convergence of the full formal series $U$ or $\\sum_\\ell B_\\ell Z^\\ell$ is required.",

        "anchor": "stmt-fin-curve",
        "exact_quote": "Let $R$ be a complete Noetherian local domain with residue field $k_n$.*\n\n1.  *Suppose $R$ is a local $k_n$-algebra and $0\\ne a\\in\\mathfrak m_R$. For every $c\\in\\mathfrak m_C\\setminus\\{0\\}$, there is a continuous $k_n$-algebra homomorphism $$R\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_C$$ sending $a$ to $c$ and $\\mathfrak m_R$ into $\\mathfrak m_C$.*\n\n2.  *Suppose $R$ is a local $W(k_n)$-algebra and $p\\ne0$ in $R$. Fix the usual embedding $W(k_n)\\hookrightarrow\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb C_p}$. There is a continuous $W(k_n)$-algebra homomorphism $$R\\longrightarrow\\mathcal O_{\\mathbb C_p}$$ sending $\\mathfrak m_R$ into $\\mathfrak m_{\\mathbb C_p}$.*\n\n*Neither homomorphism is asserted to be injective.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "Let $L$ be a complete nontrivially valued nonarchimedean field, let $r\\ge0$, and let $$\\mathscr B_r(L)=\n  \\left\\{\\sum_{I\\in\\mathbb N^r}a_I T^I:\n                    \\sup_I|a_I|<\\infty\\right\\}.$$ Equip this algebra with the coefficient norm $\\|f\\|=\\sup_I|a_I|$. For $x\\in L^r$ with $\\|x\\|<1$, substitution $T\\mapsto x+T$ defines an isometric $L$-algebra automorphism $\\tau_x$ of $\\mathscr B_r(L)$, with inverse $\\tau_{-x}$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **height ideals** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The ideals $I_h$ are the height ideals: in characteristic $p$, their vanishing specifies where the formal group has height at least $h$.
- **type of a finite spectrum** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A finite $p$-local spectrum $F$ has *type $k$* if $K(i)_*F=0$ for $i<k$ and $K(k)_*F\neq0$, with $K(0)=H\mathbb Q$.
- **thick tensor ideal** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A thick tensor ideal is a full subcategory closed under equivalences, finite sums, suspensions and desuspensions, cofiber sequences, retracts, and tensoring with every object of $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$.
- **prime thick tensor ideal** ([source](#pm-h-26)): A proper thick tensor ideal $\mathcal P$ is *prime* if $X\otimes Y\in\mathcal P$ implies $X\in\mathcal P$ or $Y\in\mathcal P$.
- **endomorphism ring and division algebra of a Honda group** ([source](#pm-h-6)): Write $$A_s=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_k(\Gamma_s),\qquad \Delta_s=A_s[1/p].$$
- **maximal ideal and maximum norm on the tilted field** ([source](#pm-h-6)): We write $\mathfrak m_C=\{c\in C:\|c\|<1\}$ and use the maximum norm on finite tuples.
- **Honda sheaf on perfectoid spaces** ([source](#pm-h-12)): For an affinoid $S=\operatorname{Spa}(R,R^+)$, let $R^{\circ\circ}$ be the topologically nilpotent elements of $R$, and put $$\mathcal H_s(S)=\Gamma_s(R^{\circ\circ}),\qquad s\geq 1.$$
- **relative section sheaf of a vector bundle** ([source](#pm-h-12)): If $\mathcal V$ is a vector bundle on $X_C$, let $\mathcal V_S$ be its pullback and define $$\mathcal B(\mathcal V)(S)=H^0(X_S,\mathcal V_S).$$
- **height-stratum parameters** ([source](#pm-h-15)): Fix for the rest of this section $$1\le h<n,\qquad r=n-h,\qquad q=p^h,\qquad Q=p^n.$$
- **height of a prime** ([source](#pm-h-26)): For $\mathfrak q\in\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(E_0)$, set $$h(\mathfrak q)=\max\{0\leq h\leq n:I_h\subseteq\mathfrak q\}.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-thick-ideal-count | number of thick tensor ideals of the dualizable K(n)-local category | = | $n+2$ | every prime and every positive height |
| q-balmer-points | number of points of the Balmer spectrum | = | $n+1$ | every prime and every positive height |
| q-division-algebra-dimension | dimension of the division algebra over the p-adic numbers | = | $s^2$ | height s at least one |
| q-stable-bundle-degree | degree of the stable bundle of rank s | = | $1$ | each rank s at least one |
| q-etale-height | étale height of the p-divisible group at the chosen point | = | $r=n-h$ | positive-characteristic height strata |
| q-level-algebra-rank | rank of the level algebra of the p^j-kernel | = | $Q^j$ | each level j |
| q-formal-subgroup-p-degree | degree of multiplication by p on a reduced formal subgroup | = | $q^e=p^{he}$ | embedding dimension e |
| q-endomorphism-algebra-dimension | dimension of the generic-fiber endomorphism algebra over the function field | = | $n^2$ | rank n |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-frobenius-separation** [claim](#stmt-formal-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-formal-separation): Choose a basis of the span of coefficients, pass to Frobenius-fixed subsequences, and apply the ultrametric inequality to the leading residue. -> Vanishing of the series obtained by applying any linear functional to the coefficients.
- **step-reduced-smooth** [claim](#stmt-formal-reduced-smooth) / [proof](#proof-stmt-formal-reduced-smooth): Compute ordinary differentials via the Frobenius decomposition, compare with the conormal module and the p-basis count, then identify dimension with embedding dimension. -> Reduced closed formal subgroups are smooth.
- **step-formal-annihilator** [claim](#stmt-formal-annihilator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-formal-annihilator): Show the ideal is Frobenius-stable, form the p-divisible subgroup of smaller height, and project a complementary summand in the semisimple isogeny category. -> A nonzero homomorphism killing the subgroup.
- **step-formal-closure** [claim](#stmt-formal-closure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-formal-closure): Show the ideal of vanishing series defines a formal subgroup by Frobenius separation and Krull intersection, then apply the annihilator lemma. -> A nonzero endomorphism relation on the subgroup.
- **step-ff-geometry** [claim](#stmt-bundles-geometry) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Cited foundational results on the Fargues–Fontaine curve. -> Geometric properties of the curve and stable bundles.
- **step-generic-independence** [claim](#stmt-bundles-independence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bundles-independence): Saturate the image, use semistability and a degree-zero determinant argument to trivialize the saturation. -> Independence at the generic point.
- **step-section-sheaf-dictionary** [claim](#stmt-bundles-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bundles-input): Combine tilting, the universal cover description, an unramified pushforward and the Banach–Colmez heart. -> Identification of Honda sheaves with section sheaves and full faithfulness.
- **step-nondegeneracy** [claim](#stmt-bundles-nondegeneracy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bundles-nondegeneracy): Translate the formal-closure relation into a bundle relation, extend to the full matrix algebra at the generic point, and select a matrix contradicting it. -> No nonzero formal equation on the orbit subgroup.
- **step-algebraization** [claim](#stmt-def-algebraization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-algebraization): Use division and Weierstrass preparation to show the level algebras are finite free and form a p-divisible group. -> Algebraic p-divisible group with the stated connected and étale parts.
- **step-specialization** [claim](#stmt-def-transport) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-transport): Establish a contraction estimate, build the limit and an explicit inverse, then check additivity and analyticity. -> The natural specialization homomorphism and cover identification.
- **step-tate-kernel** [claim](#stmt-def-tate-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-tate-kernel): Identify the kernel with compatible torsion points and use preparation to divide by p. -> Surjectivity and a free Tate lattice of the stated rank.
- **step-kodaira-spencer** [claim](#stmt-def-ks) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-ks): Apply the square-zero deformation theory of p-divisible groups and check invertibility modulo the maximal ideal via tangent injectivity. -> Isomorphism between parameter directions and marked first-order deformations.
- **step-height-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-def-height-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-height-rigidity): Use the marked Lubin–Tate classification to see that factorization forces all height parameters to vanish. -> Triviality of the deformation.
- **step-tate-coordinates** [claim](#stmt-def-coordinates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-coordinates): Lift the Tate basis to torsion sections over the dual numbers, split the étale quotient, trivialize the connected factor by rigidity, and conclude with the Kodaira–Spencer isomorphism. -> Invertible differential of the Tate coordinates.
- **step-coefficient-recursion** [claim](#stmt-def-recursion) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-recursion): Compare coefficients at multiples of the Frobenius exponent and bound roots inductively. -> Recursion and integrality of the intertwining coefficients.
- **step-natural-comparison** [claim](#stmt-def-natural-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-natural-comparison): Use integrality of the isomorphism, naturality of the specialization, and an explicit point with nonzero image. -> A nonzero natural linear morphism of Honda sheaves.
- **step-continuous-action** [claim](#stmt-def-continuous-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-continuous-action): Show that a deep congruence element lifts to an automorphism fixing the parameter map modulo a power of the maximal ideal. -> Continuity of the stabilizer action.
- **step-stabilizer-transport** [claim](#stmt-def-stabilizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-def-stabilizer): Compare the iterated limits for the two series using the contraction estimate. -> Equivariance of specialization and transport of the Tate lattice.
- **step-finite-jets** [claim](#stmt-orb-finite-jets) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-finite-jets): Truncate, use Taylor estimates for finitely many analytic germs, invert the linear part, and bound the finite sums. -> Finite-jet estimates along the sequence.
- **step-orbit-density** [claim](#stmt-orb-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-orb-finite-jets-2): Expand the germ in Tate coordinates, transport along the orbit points, take Frobenius roots, apply separation and closure, and contradict nondegeneracy. -> A germ vanishing on the orbit is zero.
- **step-auxiliary-curve** [claim](#stmt-fin-curve) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-curve): Cut down to a one-dimensional complete local domain on which the element survives, then embed a finite extension of a power series field into the algebraically closed target. -> Continuous homomorphisms into the integers of the target field.
- **step-bounded-translation** [claim](#stmt-fin-translation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-translation): Verify convergence and multiplicativity of translation coefficientwise using binomial identities. -> Translation is an isometric automorphism, so nonzero series keep nonzero germs.
- **step-charzero-orbit-germs** [claim](#stmt-fin-charzero) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-charzero): Transport the germ by the Gross–Hopkins period map to projective space, use algebra directions to get coordinates, and apply one-variable uniqueness. -> Vanishing of germs on orbits in characteristic zero.
- **step-invariant-primes** [claim](#stmt-fin-primes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-primes): Split by whether p lies in the prime, choose a point by the auxiliary curve, and contradict orbit density in each case. -> Invariant primes are zero or height ideals.
- **step-radical-ideals** [claim](#stmt-fin-radicals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-radicals): Decompose a radical ideal into finitely many minimal primes with open stabilizers and use the chain of invariant primes. -> Classification of invariant radical ideals.
- **step-invariant-radicals-theorem** [claim](#stmt-thm-invariant-radicals) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-radicals): Combine the prime classification and the radical classification. -> Stabilizer-invariant ideals are exactly the standard list.
- **step-support-comparison** [claim](#stmt-fin-support) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-support): Assemble known base-change, descent, comparison and surjectivity results to compare supports in the unrestricted direction. -> Support characterization of thick tensor ideal membership.
- **step-main-classification** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-support-2): Place all supports in the finite height chain, show each nonzero ideal attains a maximal support and is generated by a finite type spectrum, then count the strict chain. -> The chain of thick tensor ideals of length n+2.
- **step-balmer-spectrum** [claim](#stmt-fin-balmer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-fin-balmer): Show chain members are prime because supports form a total chain, then read off specialization and supports. -> The Balmer spectrum as a chain of points.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-open-subgroup-invariant-primes**: For every open subgroup of the Morava stabilizer, the proper prime ideals of the deformation ring that it stabilizes are exactly the zero ideal and the height ideals, and the radical invariant ideals are those together with the unit ideal.
- **inv-thick-ideal-chain**: The thick tensor ideals of the dualizable K(n)-local category form a single strictly decreasing chain, each term generated by a finite spectrum of the matching type and independent of that choice.
- **inv-balmer-chain**: The Balmer spectrum of the dualizable K(n)-local category consists of the proper members of the chain, with specialization given by reverse inclusion of ideals.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nondualizable-objects**: Apply the chain of thick tensor ideals to the whole K(n)-local category or to non-dualizable spectra. -> The classification is proved only inside the dualizable subcategory, which is more general than the compact objects but is not the whole local category; ideals of larger categories are not described.
- **anti-effective-procedure**: Turn the proof into an effective procedure that finds points or homomorphisms for a given invariant ideal. -> The proof uses existence of continuous homomorphisms into a valuation ring whose construction is not made explicit or injective, and uses analytic orbit arguments; no algorithm or cost bound is given.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are classification theorems proved by existence arguments in arithmetic geometry and tensor-triangular geometry: nonzero analytic germs are shown not to vanish on stabilizer orbits, and auxiliary homomorphisms into a valuation ring are only asserted to exist. No finite procedure with explicit steps over a finite input is stated, so no runnable reference implementation with an independent oracle can be built from the source.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorem list is a classification of invariant ideals and thick tensor ideals. It gives no interface, procedure or data format that a program could implement, and the proofs rely on existence and limit arguments. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The results concern nonarchimedean germs, formal groups and bundles on the Fargues–Fontaine curve. They define no dynamics, state space or discretisable model that a simulation could step through. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No finite input-to-output computation is given that a tool could wrap. The classification is a structural statement about ideals and tensor ideals, so it offers no verifiable checker for a given ideal. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The proofs build power-series limits, Frobenius roots and inverse-limit identifications over infinite objects. There is no terminating procedure with a stated cost, so no algorithm or complexity can be extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The only finite object is the chain of height ideals, a total order on n+2 ideals. It is a classification outcome, not a stored structure with operations or invariants to maintain. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the source describes arithmetic kernels, bit widths or dataflow. The valued fields involved are complete nonarchimedean and infinite, so there is no fixed-precision hardware mapping. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper states no hardness assumption, protocol or reduction. Its formal groups and division algebras are used for classification, not for a keyed construction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no data, loss, learning rule or generalisation statement. The nondegeneracy result says orbits satisfy no formal equation, which is not a statement about fitting or inference. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The source contains no objective, constraint set or feasibility question. The orbit density theorem only says an analytic germ vanishing on an orbit is zero. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No nodes, messages, faults or consensus appear. The tensor-triangular support comparison concerns support sets in a category, not concurrent processes. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite classification of thick tensor ideals could one day be formalised in a proof assistant. That would be a formalisation effort of the whole argument, and the source supplies no finite construction to implement now. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-open-subgroup** (hypothesis): The invariant-ideal classification is stated only for open subgroups of the nonextended stabilizer and for the full extended stabilizer, and not for arbitrary closed subgroups. Blocked use: ['Applying the list of invariant ideals to a non-open subgroup of the stabilizer.']
- **limit-finite-residue-field** (formal_scope): The deformation ring is the one of the Honda formal group over a finite field, so the result is the finite-residue-field formulation of the invariant-ideal conjecture. Blocked use: ['Transferring the classification to deformation rings over infinite residue fields.']
- **limit-dualizable-only** (formal_scope): The chain of thick tensor ideals classifies the dualizable K(n)-local category only; dualizability is more general than compactness but narrower than the whole local category. Blocked use: ['Reading the n+2 count as a count of thick tensor ideals of the whole K(n)-local category or of non-dualizable spectra.']
- **limit-existence-homomorphisms** (nonconstructive): The auxiliary homomorphisms into the valuation ring are only asserted to exist; no explicit construction or injectivity is claimed. Blocked use: ['Extracting an explicit procedure that finds the points used in the orbit argument.']
- **limit-height-stratum-point** (hypothesis): Local orbit density needs a point of the stratum with nonvanishing height parameter and an open subgroup orbit near that point. Blocked use: ['Applying orbit density at points where the height parameter vanishes.']
- **limit-nondegeneracy-sheaf** (hypothesis): The nondegeneracy argument needs the morphism to be a sheaf morphism and the points to be linearly independent over the p-adic numbers; it fails for arbitrary linear maps between the point groups. Blocked use: ['Using nondegeneracy for an arbitrary linear map between C-valued groups.']
- **limit-support-direction** (formal_scope): Only the support-descent direction of the comparison is used, which holds at every prime; the converse direction, using realization of Morava modules, is not claimed. Blocked use: ['Assuming the converse realization of arbitrary Morava modules for all primes.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We classify the prime and radical ideals of the Lubin–Tate deformation ring invariant under an open Morava stabilizer subgroup, at every prime and positive height. This proves Chai's invariant-ideal conjecture in the standard finite-residue-field formulation. It also proves the Hovey–Strickland conjecture: the dualizable $`K(n)`$-local category has exactly $`n+2`$ thick tensor ideals, and its Balmer spectrum is a chain of $`n+1`$ points.

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Chromatic height organizes both the deformation theory of formal groups and the tensor structure of stable homotopy theory. Two classification problems ask whether height accounts for all the relevant closed subobjects: the invariant ideals in a Lubin–Tate deformation ring, and the thick tensor ideals of dualizable Morava-local spectra. We prove both classifications, using the arithmetic problem as the main step.

Fix a prime $p$ and an integer $n\geq1$. Let $\Gamma_n$ be the height-$n$ Honda formal group, let $A_n$ be its endomorphism ring over $\overline{\mathbb F}_p$, and put $$E_0=W(\mathbb F_{p^n})[[u_1,\ldots,u_{n-1}]],
 \qquad I_0=0,\qquad
 I_h=(p,u_1,\ldots,u_{h-1})\quad(1\leq h\leq n).$$ The ring $E_0$ represents its marked deformations. The ideals $I_h$ are the height ideals: in characteristic $p$, their vanishing specifies where the formal group has height at least $h$. The nonextended Morava stabilizer $A_n^\times$ acts by changing the marking. The canonical extended stabilizer $G_n$ also includes the residue-field Galois action. The unit ideal is listed separately from the proper ideals.

<a id="stmt-thm-invariant-radicals"></a>
<div id="thm:invariant-radicals" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Stabilizer-invariant ideals). *Let $U\subseteq A_n^\times$ be any open subgroup. The proper prime ideals of $E_0$ stabilized by $U$ are precisely $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n.$$ The $U$-invariant radical ideals are precisely $$0,\ I_1,\ldots,I_n,\ E_0.$$ The latter list also gives the radical ideals invariant under the full canonical $G_n$-action.*

</div>

This proves the invariant-ideal assertion called Chai’s Hope in the finite-residue-field formulation of (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Hope 4.1 and Remark 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)], and extends that formulation from the full stabilizer to every open subgroup. The open-subgroup scope also occurs in Chai’s original closed-fiber question, as explained in (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Remark 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)]. The second classification concerns Honda Morava $E$-theory $E$ with coefficient ring $E_0$, and the homotopy category $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$ of dualizable $K(n)$-local spectra. Its tensor product and unit are $$X\otimes Y=L_{K(n)}(X\wedge Y),
 \qquad \mathbf 1=L_{K(n)}S.$$ A thick tensor ideal is a full subcategory closed under equivalences, finite sums, suspensions and desuspensions, cofiber sequences, retracts, and tensoring with every object of $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$. Dualizability is essential here: it is more general than compactness in the $K(n)$-local category (Hovey and Strickland 1999, Theorems 8.5–8.6) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[HS99](#ref-HS99)].

A finite $p$-local spectrum $F$ has *type $k$* if $K(i)_*F=0$ for $i<k$ and $K(k)_*F\neq0$, with $K(0)=H\mathbb Q$. Such spectra exist in every type: start with the sphere and the degree-$p$ cofiber, and then take cofibers of the self-maps supplied by the Hopkins–Smith periodicity theorem (Hopkins and Smith 1998, Definition 8 and Theorem 9) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[HopkinsSmith98](#ref-HopkinsSmith98)]. For $0\leq k\leq n$, choose a finite type-$k$ spectrum $F(k)$ and put $$\mathcal D_k=\big\langle L_{K(n)}F(k)\big\rangle_\otimes,
 \qquad \mathcal D_{n+1}=0,$$ where the brackets mean the generated thick tensor ideal in $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 2** (Hovey–Strickland conjecture). *For every prime $p$ and every $n\geq 1$, the thick tensor ideals of $\mathcal D_{(n,p)}$ are exactly $$\mathcal D_{(n,p)}=\mathcal D_0
 \supsetneq\mathcal D_1\supsetneq\cdots
 \supsetneq\mathcal D_n\supsetneq\mathcal D_{n+1}=0.$$ In particular, there are $n+2$ such ideals, and each $\mathcal D_k$ is independent of the chosen finite type-$k$ spectrum.*

</div>

The Balmer spectrum of a tensor category is the space of its prime thick tensor ideals. Corollary <a href="#fin:balmer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fin:balmer">30</a> identifies this spectrum here with the $n+1$ proper members of the displayed chain, including the zero ideal. It also specifies their specialization order and the support of each finite type generator.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### History and the two classification problems

The thick subcategory theorem of Hopkins and Smith classifies thick subcategories of finite spectra by chromatic type, while their periodicity theorem supplies the corresponding self-maps (Hopkins and Smith 1998, Theorems 7 and 9) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[HopkinsSmith98](#ref-HopkinsSmith98)]. Hovey and Strickland proposed the dualizable $K(n)$-local counterpart in their study of Morava $K$-theory and localization (Hovey and Strickland 1999, sec. 12, p. 61) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[HS99](#ref-HS99)]. Their Problem 16.8 also asks directly about stabilizer-invariant primes and radical ideals of the deformation ring; it explains why the invariant-prime theorem for ordinary complex cobordism does not by itself answer this question.

Chai studied the action on the closed fiber of Lubin–Tate space and proposed that invariant irreducible formal loci should be height loci (Chai 1996) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Chai96](#ref-Chai96)]. We use the precise formulation and comparison of coefficient fields and group actions in (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, sec. 4, Hope 4.1 and Remark 4.2) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)]. Formal rigidity also has an established role in Chai’s work on $p$-divisible formal groups (Chai 2008) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[Chai08](#ref-Chai08)]. The formal-closure argument below belongs to this general circle of ideas, but starts from a subgroup of Honda-group points and proves its required annihilator statement directly.

Barthel, Heard, and Naumann proved the Hovey–Strickland conjecture at height two for every prime and established the general implication from Chai’s invariant-ideal assertion to the spectral classification (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Theorems 4.9 and 4.15) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)]. The descent and support methods in that implication build on Mathew’s work on Morava $E$-theory descent (Mathew 2016) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[Mathew16](#ref-Mathew16)] and Balmer’s tensor-nilpotence criterion for surjectivity on spectra (Balmer 2018) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[Balmer18](#ref-Balmer18)]. We recall the exact support comparison in Proposition <a href="#fin:support" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fin:support">29</a>. Its direction is unrestricted in $p$. The inequality $2p-2>n^2+n$ in (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Theorem 4.13) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)] concerns the converse, which uses realization of Morava modules.

The generic-fiber part has earlier Lie-theoretic precedents. Barthel–Heard–Naumann credit Chai with an unpublished characteristic-zero invariant-ideal result and use the Gross–Hopkins period map to study projective stabilizer orbits (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Remark 4.2 and Proposition 4.18) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)]. We retain a local analytic-germ proof of the needed conclusion. The main issue addressed here is the characteristic-$p$ height strata, where infinitesimal stabilizer directions alone do not give the required control of invariant equations.

Recent generation and comparison results concern related categories. Lee and Pstrągowski identify the category of locally fp spectra, which contains the dualizable $K(n)$-local spectra as well as nondualizable objects (Lee and Pstrągowski 2026, Example 1.6 and Theorems 1.7–1.9) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[LP26](#ref-LP26)]. Surjectivity of the map from the $K(n)$-local to the $E(n)$-local Balmer spectrum was established by Barthel–Heard–Naumann (Barthel, Heard, and Naumann 2022, Remark 3.6) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[BHN22](#ref-BHN22)]; Balmer–Sanders give a later general treatment (Balmer and Sanders 2025, Proposition 8.11 and Remark 8.13) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[BalmerSanders](#ref-BalmerSanders)]. These results provide generation and surjectivity statements. The arithmetic theorem above classifies the invariant supports needed to determine the entire ideal lattice.

<figure id="fig:proof-flow">

<figcaption>The two orbit arguments classify invariant primes. Only the characteristic-$p$ branch uses the formal, bundle, and deformation constructions of Sections <a href="#sec:formal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:formal">2</a>–<a href="#sec:deformations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:deformations">4</a>. The arithmetic branches join before the all-prime support comparison.</figcaption>
</figure>

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The argument

Set $C=\mathbb C_p^\flat$ and $k=\overline{\mathbb F}_p$, viewed as the coefficient field in $C$, and write $\mathfrak m_C=\{c\in C:|c|<1\}$. Fix a characteristic-$p$ point $x$ of formal height $1\leq h<n$. The main task is to show that a nonzero analytic germ at $x$ on the height-at-least-$h$ parameter locus $I_h=0$ cannot vanish on the stabilizer orbit of $x$. The finite kernels of the universal deformation give a height-$n$ algebraic $p$-divisible group. At $x$, its connected part has height $h$ and its étale quotient has height $r=n-h$. The universal-cover comparison lets us regard a basis $z_1,\ldots,z_r$ of its étale Tate module as points of the perfected height-$n$ Honda group. Specializing these fixed vectors at nearby parameters in this locus gives analytic functions $\theta_i$ vanishing at $x$. Their vanishing tests whether the vectors remain in the nearby Tate lattice. Section <a href="#sec:deformations" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:deformations">4</a> proves that their parameter differential is invertible: a vector in its kernel splits the étale quotient over the dual numbers, fixed-height rigidity makes the connected factor constant, and the Kodaira–Spencer map then kills the parameter vector (Proposition <a href="#def:coordinates" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:coordinates">17</a>).

Put $q=p^h$. For a fixed $d\in A_n$, let $y_N=(1-p^Nd)\cdot x$; these points tend to $x$. Stabilizer transport relates $\theta_i(y_N)$ to multiplication by $p^N$ in the formal group at $y_N$. Integral comparison series intertwining multiplication by $p$ with that of the height-$h$ Honda group turn this relation into coordinatewise Frobenius powers. Applying these series to the specializations of $z_i$ and $dz_i$ at $y_N$ gives tuples $U_N$ and $s_N$, respectively, in $\mathfrak m_C^r$. Theorem <a href="#orb:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:density">22</a> proves the exact identity and limit $$U_N=s_N^{q^N},\qquad
 s_N\longrightarrow s(d)=\bigl(\nu_x(dz_1),\ldots,\nu_x(dz_r)\bigr),$$ where $\nu_x$ is specialization at $x$ followed by an integral formal identification with the height-$h$ Honda group. Proposition <a href="#def:natural-comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:natural-comparison">19</a> makes $\nu_x$ a nonzero natural map on perfectoid test spaces. The corresponding change of parameter coordinates is formal. To compare it with the numerical tuples $U_N$, we match any fixed finite number of analytic coefficient germs and bound the remaining integral tails (Lemma <a href="#orb:finite-jets" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:finite-jets">23</a>). The coefficient germs may have shrinking domains; the estimates require only one finite jet at a time.

Suppose a nonzero analytic germ $P$ vanishes on the orbit. Its expansion in the formal coordinates has fixed coefficients $B_\ell$, chosen before $d$. Finite truncation followed by $q^N$-th roots gives bounds on the finite sums $\sum_{|\ell|\leq m} B_\ell^{1/q^N}s(d)^\ell$. Frobenius separation (Lemma <a href="#formal:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="formal:separation">3</a>) turns these bounds into a single nonzero formal equation over $k$ on the subgroup $$S_x=\{\bigl(\nu_x(dz_1),\ldots,\nu_x(dz_r)\bigr):d\in A_n\}.$$ The formal-closure argument in Section <a href="#sec:formal" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:formal">2</a> then produces a nonzero row of Honda endomorphisms annihilating $S_x$ (Proposition <a href="#formal:closure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="formal:closure">7</a>).

The contradiction is geometric. The Fargues–Fontaine curve (Fargues and Fontaine 2018) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[FF18](#ref-FF18)] and Le Bras’s theorem on Banach–Colmez sheaves (Le Bras 2018) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[LeBras18](#ref-LeBras18)] turn this natural endomorphism relation into a vector-bundle relation. A degree-one determinant argument promotes the $\mathbb Q_p$-independence of $z_1,\ldots,z_r$ to independence at the generic point of the curve (Lemma <a href="#bundles:independence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bundles:independence">9</a>). There the division algebra action spans the full matrix algebra. A matrix selecting one of these independent vectors contradicts the nonzero endomorphism row (Proposition <a href="#bundles:nondegeneracy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="bundles:nondegeneracy">11</a>). Thus $P$ must be zero. This vanishing property is the *local orbit density* proved in Theorem <a href="#orb:density" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="orb:density">22</a>.

Section <a href="#sec:classification" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:classification">6</a> uses this result to classify invariant primes containing $p$. Primes in characteristic zero are handled separately by the Gross–Hopkins period map (Hopkins and Gross 1994b, 1994a) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[GH94](#ref-GH94), [HG94Bundles](#ref-HG94Bundles)]. A finite minimal-prime argument classifies invariant radical ideals, and the established support comparison transfers the height chain to thick tensor ideals and their Balmer spectrum. The final description also identifies the support map from $\mathop{\mathrm{Spec}}(E_0)$: each height stratum maps to one Balmer point. Figure <a href="#fig:proof-flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="fig:proof-flow">1</a> records the two arithmetic branches and where they enter the spectral argument.

<a id="sec:formal"></a>
### Frobenius separation and formal closure

We first establish a rigidity statement for subgroups of products of a Honda formal group. A formal equation over the residue field will force such a subgroup to satisfy a nonzero homomorphism equation. The first step separates coefficients by taking increasingly deep Frobenius roots.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Fields and Honda formal groups

Fix a prime $p$. Throughout the proof, put $$k=\overline{\mathbb F_p},\qquad C=\mathbb C_p^{\flat},$$ and fix the coefficient-field embedding $k\hookrightarrow\mathcal O_C$ inducing the identification of the residue field with $k$. The field $C$ is algebraically closed, complete, and of characteristic $p$. We write $\mathfrak m_C=\{c\in C:|c|<1\}$ and use the maximum norm on finite tuples. Every nonzero element of $k$ has norm one. Consequently a formal power series over $k$ can be evaluated at any tuple in $\mathfrak m_C^r$.

For $s\geq 1$, set $q_s=p^s$ and $k_s=\mathbb F_{q_s}$. The Lubin–Tate formal-module construction for the unramified extension of $\mathbb Q_p$ of degree $s$, applied to the series $pT+T^{q_s}$ (Lubin and Tate 1965, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[LT65](#ref-LT65)], gives on reduction a one-dimensional commutative Honda formal group $\Gamma_s$ over $k_s$ with $$[p]_{\Gamma_s}(T)=T^{q_s}.$$ Write $$A_s=\mathop{\mathrm{End}}_k(\Gamma_s),\qquad \Delta_s=A_s[1/p].$$ The classification of one-dimensional formal groups (Hazewinkel 1978, Theorem 18.5.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[Hazewinkel78](#ref-Hazewinkel78)] identifies every formal group of height $s$ over an algebraically closed field of characteristic $p$ with $\Gamma_s$. Dieudonné theory over perfect fields (Demazure 1972, vol. 302, chap. III, §8; Chapter IV, §§1,3–4) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Demazure72](#ref-Demazure72)] shows that its associated $p$-divisible group is simple up to isogeny and that $\Delta_s$ is a central division algebra over $\mathbb Q_p$ of dimension $s^2$. The isogeny category of $p$-divisible groups over $k$ is semisimple. These statements include height one and the prime two.

Every endomorphism of $\Gamma_s$ is defined over $k_s$. Indeed, if $a(T)=\sum_{j\geq1}a_jT^j$ is such an endomorphism, its commutation with multiplication by $p$ gives $$a(T^{q_s})=a(T)^{q_s},$$ so $a_j^{q_s}=a_j$ for all $j$. We use formal-group addition in $\Gamma_s$ and its products; ordinary sums of power-series coefficients and ordinary differences of coordinates retain their usual meaning.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Separating coefficients

For a multi-index $\ell=(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r)\in\mathbb N^r$, put $|\ell|=\sum_i\ell_i$ and $T^\ell=\prod_i T_i^{\ell_i}$. Roots under Frobenius in $C$ are unique. For a related separation argument using high Frobenius substitutions, see (Chai 2008, Proposition 3.1) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Chai08](#ref-Chai08)].

<a id="stmt-formal-separation"></a>
<div id="formal:separation" class="lemma">

<a id="formal:root-bound"></a>
**Lemma 3** (Frobenius separation). *Let $h,r\geq1$, put $q=p^h$, and let $(b_\ell)_{\ell\in\mathbb N^r}$ be an arbitrary family in $C$. Suppose $s\in\mathfrak m_C^r$ satisfies, for every integer $m\geq0$, $$\label{formal:root-bound}
  \limsup_{N\longrightarrow\infty}
  \left|\sum_{|\ell|\leq m} b_\ell^{1/q^N}s^\ell\right|
  \leq \|s\|^{m+1}.$$ Then, for every $k$-linear functional $\lambda:C\to k$, $$\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb N^r}\lambda(b_\ell)s^\ell=0.$$ The series in this conclusion converges, and no continuity assumption on $\lambda$ is required.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-formal-separation"><summary>Proof of stmt-formal-separation</summary>

*Proof.* Fix $m$. If all the coefficients through degree $m$ vanish, the corresponding assertion is immediate. Otherwise choose a $k$-basis $\beta_1,\ldots,\beta_a$ for their span and write $$b_\ell=\sum_{j=1}^a c_{\ell j}\beta_j,
  \qquad c_{\ell j}\in k\quad (|\ell|\leq m).$$ There is a subsequence of positive integers $N$ on which $q^N$-power Frobenius fixes all the $c_{\ell j}$: finitely many elements of $\overline{\mathbb F_p}$ lie in a common finite field. On this subsequence, $$\sum_{|\ell|\leq m}b_\ell^{1/q^N}s^\ell
  =\sum_{j=1}^a\beta_j^{1/q^N}v_j,
  \qquad v_j=\sum_{|\ell|\leq m}c_{\ell j}s^\ell.$$ Put $V=\max_j|v_j|$. If $V>0$, choose $j_0$ with $|v_{j_0}|=V$, set $w_j=v_j/v_{j_0}$, and let $\rho_j\in k$ be the residue of $w_j\in\mathcal O_C$. At least one $\rho_j$ is nonzero, and $$\eta:=\max_j|w_j-\rho_j|<1.$$ Pass to a further subsequence on which $q^N$-power Frobenius also fixes all the $\rho_j$. The independence of the $\beta_j$ gives $\sum_j\beta_j\rho_j\ne0$, whence $$\left|\sum_j\beta_j^{1/q^N}\rho_j\right|
  =\left|\sum_j\beta_j\rho_j\right|^{1/q^N}
  \longrightarrow1.$$ On the other hand, $$\limsup_N
  \left|\sum_j\beta_j^{1/q^N}(w_j-\rho_j)\right|
  \leq\eta<1,$$ since $|\beta_j|^{1/q^N}\to1$ for every $j$. The ultrametric inequality therefore implies $$\left|\sum_j\beta_j^{1/q^N}v_j\right|\longrightarrow V$$ along this subsequence. Equation <a href="#formal:root-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:root-bound">[formal:root-bound]</a> gives $V\leq\|s\|^{m+1}$; this inequality also holds if $V=0$.

For an arbitrary $k$-linear functional $\lambda$, we now have the finite-sum estimate $$\left|\sum_{|\ell|\leq m}\lambda(b_\ell)s^\ell\right|
  =\left|\sum_j\lambda(\beta_j)v_j\right|
  \leq V\leq\|s\|^{m+1}.$$ Here $|\lambda(\beta_j)|\leq1$, because these values lie in $k$. The series with coefficients $\lambda(b_\ell)\in k$ converges at $s$, and the last bound tends to zero as $m\to\infty$. At $s=0$, Equation <a href="#formal:root-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:root-bound">[formal:root-bound]</a> for $m=0$ forces $b_0=0$, giving the same conclusion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-formal-separation-quantifiers"></a>
<div id="formal:separation-quantifiers" class="remark">

*Remark 4*. The family $b_\ell$ need not be bounded. All uses of $\lambda$ in the proof concern finite sums. Moreover, the conclusion holds for *every* $\lambda$ at each tuple satisfying the hypothesis. Thus, if the same family satisfies Equation <a href="#formal:root-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:root-bound">[formal:root-bound]</a> at every point of a set, one may choose a single $\lambda$ nonzero on one prescribed nonzero $b_\ell$ and obtain a common nonzero formal equation over $k$ on that set. The Frobenius subsequences used to prove the estimates may depend on the tuple and on $m$.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Reduced formal subgroups

To prove formal closure, we will show that the ideal of all equations vanishing on a subgroup of points defines a reduced formal subgroup. The next two lemmas turn that formal subgroup into a nonzero homomorphism equation. We first prove smoothness, before its dimension or irreducibility is known. The use of ordinary differentials is justified by the finite Frobenius expansion over a perfect coefficient field.

<a id="stmt-formal-reduced-smooth"></a>
<div id="formal:reduced-smooth" class="lemma">

**Lemma 5**. *Let $\mathcal G$ be a smooth formal group over $k$ of dimension $r$, with coordinate ring $R=k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$. If $\mathcal H\subseteq\mathcal G$ is a reduced closed formal subgroup, then $\mathcal H$ is smooth. More precisely, for $B=R/I$, with augmentation ideal $\mathfrak m_B$, put $e=\dim_k(\mathfrak m_B/\mathfrak m_B^2)$. Then $B\cong k[[X_1,\ldots,X_e]]$ as a complete local $k$-algebra.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-formal-reduced-smooth"><summary>Proof of stmt-formal-reduced-smooth</summary>

<a id="formal:differential-presentation"></a>
*Proof.* Every $f\in R$ has a unique finite decomposition $$f=\sum_{0\leq a_i<p}T_1^{a_1}\cdots T_r^{a_r}f_a(T)^p.$$ An ordinary $k$-derivation kills all the $p$th powers, so this identity shows that it is determined by its values on the $T_i$, through the usual formal partial derivatives. Those partial derivatives themselves are derivations. Hence the ordinary module $\Omega^1_{R/k}$ is free on $dT_1,\ldots,dT_r$, and the quotient presentation gives $$\label{formal:differential-presentation}
  \Omega^1_{B/k}
  \cong B^r\big/
  \left\langle
    \bigl(\partial_1f,\ldots,\partial_rf\bigr):f\in I
  \right\rangle_B.$$ In particular, this ordinary differential module is finite over $B$.

<a id="formal:invariant-differentials"></a>
Consider the completed product ring $$B\widehat\otimes_k B
  =k[[X_1,\ldots,X_r,Y_1,\ldots,Y_r]]/(I(X),I(Y))$$ and its diagonal ideal $J=(Y_i-X_i)_i$. Writing $Y=X+Z$ modulo $(Z)^2$ gives $$f(X+Z)=f(X)+\sum_i\partial_i f(X)Z_i\pmod{(Z)^2}.$$ Thus $J/J^2$ has exactly the presentation in Equation <a href="#formal:differential-presentation" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:differential-presentation">[formal:differential-presentation]</a>. This computation makes no reducedness assumption on the completed product. The group-difference automorphism $$(x,y)\longmapsto(x,y-_{\mathcal H}x)$$ carries the diagonal to the identity section in the second factor. The conormal module of that section is $B\otimes_k(\mathfrak m_B/\mathfrak m_B^2)$: it is obtained by retaining just the linear terms in the second coordinates. We conclude that $$\label{formal:invariant-differentials}
  \Omega^1_{B/k}\cong
  B\otimes_k(\mathfrak m_B/\mathfrak m_B^2)\cong B^e.$$

Let $\mathfrak p$ be a minimal prime of $B$ and set $D=B/\mathfrak p$, $K=\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(D)$, and $d=\dim D$. Since $B$ is reduced, $B_{\mathfrak p}$ is the field $K$. Localization of ordinary differentials in Equation <a href="#formal:invariant-differentials" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:invariant-differentials">[formal:invariant-differentials]</a> gives $\dim_K\Omega^1_{K/k}=e$.

Choose a system of parameters in the complete local domain $D$. Completeness and the finite-dimensional quotient by those parameters give a finite map $k[[x_1,\ldots,x_d]]\to D$. It is injective by dimension, since a nonzero ideal in the source would lower its dimension. Thus $K$ is a finite extension of $L=\mathop{\mathrm{Frac}}(k[[x_1,\ldots,x_d]])$. Monomials with exponents below $p$ form a basis over Frobenius, so $[L:L^p]=p^d$. This degree is unchanged by the finite extension: Frobenius identifies the extensions $K/L$ and $K^p/L^p$, and comparison of the two towers over $L^p$ gives $$[K:K^p]
  =\frac{[K:L]\,[L:L^p]}{[K^p:L^p]}=p^d.$$ This argument includes inseparable extensions. Because $k$ is perfect, the $p$-basis description of field differentials now gives $\dim_K\Omega^1_{K/k}=d$. Indeed, a $p$-basis of $K$ over $K^p$ has $d$ elements, and their differentials form a $K$-basis of $\Omega^1_{K/k}$. Therefore $d=e$.

Every component has dimension $e$, so $\dim B=e$. This equals the embedding dimension of $B$, making $B$ regular. Lifting a basis of $\mathfrak m_B/\mathfrak m_B^2$ gives a surjection $k[[X_1,\ldots,X_e]]\to B$ by completeness. Equality of dimensions makes its kernel zero, proving the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-formal-annihilator"></a>
<div id="formal:annihilator" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6**. *Let $h,r\geq1$. Every proper reduced closed formal subgroup $\mathcal H\subsetneq\Gamma_h^r$ is annihilated by a nonzero homomorphism $\Gamma_h^r\to\Gamma_h$ defined over $k$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-formal-annihilator"><summary>Proof of stmt-formal-annihilator</summary>

*Proof.* Put $q=p^h$, and let $I\ne0$ be the defining ideal of $\mathcal H$ in $R=k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$. By Lemma <a href="#formal:reduced-smooth" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="formal:reduced-smooth">5</a>, its coordinate ring is a power-series ring $B$ of some dimension $e<r$. If $e=0$, then $B=k$ and $\mathcal H$ is the origin; a coordinate projection proves the assertion. Assume henceforth that $e>0$.

Let $\sigma:R\to R$ raise every coefficient to its $q$th power and fix the variables. For $P\in I$, stability under multiplication by $p$ gives $$P(T_1^q,\ldots,T_r^q)=(\sigma^{-1}P(T))^q\in I.$$ Radicality implies $\sigma^{-1}I\subseteq I$. Applying $\sigma$ repeatedly produces the ascending chain $$I\subseteq\sigma I\subseteq\sigma^2 I\subseteq\cdots.$$ It stabilizes because $R$ is Noetherian, and applying an inverse iterate of $\sigma$ to a stationary pair gives $I=\sigma I$. Consequently $\sigma$ descends to an automorphism of $B$.

The coordinate pullback of $[p]$ on $\mathcal H$ is the $h$-fold absolute Frobenius of $B$, composed with $\sigma^{-1}$. This $h$-fold Frobenius on $B\cong k[[X_1,\ldots,X_e]]$ is finite free, with basis given by monomials whose exponents are less than $q$. Thus $[p]$ on $\mathcal H$ is finite flat of degree $q^e=p^{he}$. The kernels $\mathcal H[p^j]$ are finite flat of order $p^{hej}$. The map induced by $[p]$ from level $j+1$ to level $j$ is a pullback of $[p]:\mathcal H\to\mathcal H$, hence is faithfully flat. These kernels form a $p$-divisible subgroup $\mathcal H[p^\infty]$ of $\Gamma_h^r[p^\infty]$, of height $he<hr$.

<a id="formal:kernel-topology"></a>
They also recover the formal topology. If $\overline T_i$ are the images of the ambient coordinates in $B$ and $$J_j=(\overline T_1^{q^j},\ldots,\overline T_r^{q^j}),$$ then $$\label{formal:kernel-topology}
  \mathfrak m_B^{r(q^j-1)+1}\subseteq J_j
  \subseteq\mathfrak m_B^{q^j}.$$ The first inclusion follows because any monomial of the indicated total degree has an exponent at least $q^j$.

In the semisimple isogeny category over $k$, the ambient group is a sum of $r$ copies of the simple object associated to $\Gamma_h$. The subgroup has smaller height. Projecting a nonzero complementary summand onto one simple factor gives a nonzero rational morphism $$\varphi_{\mathbb Q_p}:\Gamma_h^r[p^\infty]
  \longrightarrow\Gamma_h[p^\infty]$$ whose restriction to $\mathcal H[p^\infty]$ is zero. Choose one common power of $p$ clearing its denominator, and let $\varphi$ be the resulting actual morphism of $p$-divisible groups. Its restriction is actually zero. To see this, Hom groups of $p$-divisible groups are $p$-torsion-free: if $p^a f=0$, then $f\circ[p]^a=0$, and $[p]^a$ on the source is an epimorphism of fppf sheaves. A rationally zero restriction is killed by one power of $p$ in its Hom group and hence vanishes.

The compatible coordinate maps at finite levels recover an actual formal homomorphism $\Gamma_h^r\to\Gamma_h$. In fact, the ambient and target kernel ideals are respectively $(T_1^{q^j},\ldots,T_r^{q^j})$ and $(T^{q^j})$, and inverse limits of their coordinate rings are the corresponding power-series rings. The finite-level homomorphism identities pass to these separated inverse limits. The resulting formal morphism is nonzero since $\varphi$ is nonzero. Its restriction to $\mathcal H$ vanishes by Equation <a href="#formal:kernel-topology" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="formal:kernel-topology">[formal:kernel-topology]</a> and $\bigcap_j J_j=0$. This proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### From an equation to an annihilator

<a id="stmt-formal-closure"></a>
<div id="formal:closure" class="proposition">

**Proposition 7** (Formal closure). *Let $h,r\geq1$, and let $S$ be a subgroup of $\Gamma_h^r(\mathfrak m_C)$. If a nonzero power series in $k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$ vanishes on $S$, then there are $a_1,\ldots,a_r\in A_h$, not all zero, such that $$a_1(s_1)+_{\Gamma_h}\cdots+_{\Gamma_h}a_r(s_r)=0
  \qquad\text{for every }s\in S.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-formal-closure"><summary>Proof of stmt-formal-closure</summary>

*Proof.* Let $R=k[[T_1,\ldots,T_r]]$, let $F$ be the law of $\Gamma_h^r$, and let $I\subset R$ be the ideal of all series vanishing on $S$. Evaluation into the field $C$ shows that $I$ is radical. It is nonzero by hypothesis and proper because $0\in S$. We show that it defines a formal subgroup.

For $P\in I$, put $Q(T,T')=P(F(T,T'))$. Fix $s\in S$ and write $$Q(s,T')=\sum_\ell b_\ell(T')^\ell.$$ Each $b_\ell$ is the evaluation at $s$ of a series over $k$, so $|b_\ell|\leq1$. For $q=p^h$ and every $t\in S$, coordinatewise $q^N$th power is multiplication by $p^N$; hence $Q(s,t^{q^N})=0$. Truncation at degree $m$ gives $$\left|\sum_{|\ell|\leq m}b_\ell t^{q^N\ell}\right|
  \leq\|t\|^{q^N(m+1)}.$$ Taking $q^N$th roots and applying Lemma <a href="#formal:separation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="formal:separation">3</a> shows that, for every $k$-linear $\lambda:C\to k$, the coefficientwise transform $$Q_{s,\lambda}(T'):=\sum_\ell\lambda(b_\ell)(T')^\ell$$ vanishes on $S$, and therefore belongs to $I(T')$.

To descend this statement in the first variable, fix $m$ and consider the finite-dimensional $k$-algebra $$D_m=k[[T']]/\bigl(I(T')+(T')^{m+1}\bigr).$$ Choose a $k$-basis $e_1,\ldots,e_b$ of $D_m$. Modulo its defining ideal, write $$Q(T,T')=\sum_{a=1}^b c_a(T)e_a,
  \qquad c_a(T)\in k[[T]].$$ Every $c_a(T)$ is a finite $k$-linear combination of the coefficients of $(T')^\ell$ in $Q$, with $|\ell|\leq m$. Consequently $\lambda(c_a(s))$ is exactly the $e_a$-coordinate of the image of $Q_{s,\lambda}$ in $D_m$. It is zero. Since algebraic $k$-linear functionals separate the points of $C$, we obtain $c_a(s)=0$ for every $s\in S$, and thus $c_a\in I(T)$. This argument moves $\lambda$ across a finite linear combination of already evaluated coefficients; it does not move it through an infinite evaluation.

It follows that $$Q\in I(T)+I(T')+(T')^{m+1}
  \qquad\text{for every }m.$$ Krull intersection (The Stacks Project Authors 2026, Tag 00IP) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[Stacks](#ref-Stacks)] in the Noetherian local quotient by $I(T)+I(T')$ gives $Q\in I(T)+I(T')$. The identity and inversion conditions follow from $0\in S$ and $-_{\Gamma_h^r}S=S$. Therefore $\mathop{\mathrm{Spf}}(R/I)$ is a proper reduced closed formal subgroup of $\Gamma_h^r$.

Lemma <a href="#formal:annihilator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="formal:annihilator">6</a> supplies a nonzero formal homomorphism $\varphi:\Gamma_h^r\to\Gamma_h$ killing this subgroup. Its restrictions to the factors are endomorphisms $a_i\in A_h$, and the commutative group law gives $$\varphi(T)=a_1(T_1)+_{\Gamma_h}\cdots+_{\Gamma_h}a_r(T_r).$$ They are not all zero. The coordinate series of $\varphi$ belongs to $I$, so evaluating on $S$ proves the required identity. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:bundles"></a>
### A nondegeneracy lemma from the Fargues–Fontaine curve

The formal-closure result reduces our problem to endomorphism relations among Honda-group points. We first prove the geometric fact that will exclude those relations: fewer than $n$ global sections of the rank-$n$, degree-one stable bundle, independent over $\mathbb Q_p$, remain independent at the generic point. We then identify Honda-group points with sections of these bundles. The identification holds on every perfectoid test space, so natural maps and endomorphisms become bundle maps.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Degree-one bundles and independent sections

Write $X_C$ for the algebraic Fargues–Fontaine curve with coefficient field $\mathbb Q_p$. We use the vector bundle GAGA equivalence to pass between its algebraic and adic versions. The following geometric properties are the inputs to the determinant argument.

<a id="stmt-bundles-geometry"></a>
<div id="bundles:geometry" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Fargues–Fontaine). *The curve $X_C$ is integral, regular and Noetherian of dimension one, and $H^0(X_C,\mathcal O_{X_C})=\mathbb Q_p$. Degrees of vector bundles are integers, and every nonzero effective divisor has positive degree. For each $s\geq1$ there is a stable bundle $$\mathcal E_s=\mathcal O_{X_C}(1/s)$$ of rank $s$ and degree one.*

</div>
